You will be assessed on this consistency.\n    14→\n    15→---\n    16→\n    17→## Project Context\n    18→\n    19→- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n    20→- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference papers and code repository. \n    21→- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n    22→- We will assess your correctness on **matching the loading and processing described in the reference texts and code**. \n    23→\n    24→## Reference Information\n    25→\n    26→- The reference **papers**:\n    27→  - \"A brain-wide map of neural activity during complex behaviour\" describes how the data were collected and is in the file `datapaper.pdf`.\n    28→  - \"Exploiting correlations across trials and behavioral sessions to improve neural decoding\" describes how the code were used and is in the file `methodpaper.pdf`.\n    29→  - A white paper about the data architecture is in `dataarchitecture.pdf\n    30→- Some parts of the papers that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `methods.txt`. \n    31→- **Code** from the methods paper are in the directory `code`.\n    32→- **Data** from the data paper are in the directory `data`. Some data may be missing and may need to be downloaded. \n    33→- **Documentation** about how to access the data can be found at https://docs.internationalbrainlab.org/public_docs/public_introduction.html.\n    34→\n    35→## Decoder Task\n    36→\n    37→Temporally align based on stimulus onset\n    38→\n    39→### Decoder Inputs:\n    40→- Time since stimulus onset, continuous, time-varying\n    41→- Trial number in block, continuous, per-trial\n    42→\n    43→### Decoder Outputs\n    44→- Choice, binary, per-trial, left = 0, right = 1\n    45→- Prior probability of left, per-trial, 0.2 -> 0, 0.5 -> 1, 0.8 -> 2\n    46→- Wheel speed discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\n    47→- Whisker motion energy discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\n    48→\n    49→## Target Data Format\n    50→\n    51→- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n    52→- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n    53→\n    54→```python\n    55→data = {\n    56→    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n    57→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    58→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n    59→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n    60→            ...\n    61→        ],\n    62→        [  # list of trials for session 2\n    63→            ...\n    64→        ],\n    65→        ...\n    66→    ],\n    67→\n    68→    'input': [  # list of sessions\n    69→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    70→            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n    71→            ...\n    72→        ],\n    73→        ...\n    74→    ],\n    75→\n    76→    'output': [  # list of sessions\n    77→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    78→            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n    79→            ...\n    80→        ],\n    81→        ...\n    82→    ],\n    83→\n    84→    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    85→    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n    86→\n    87→    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    88→    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n    89→        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    90→    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n    91→\n    92→    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    93→    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    94→    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n    95→        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n    96→        ...\n    97→    ],\n    98→\n    99→    'metadata': {\n   100→        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n   101→        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n   102→        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n   103→        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   104→        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   105→        ...\n   106→    }\n   107→}\n   108→```\n   109→\n   110→### Key Format Requirements\n   111→\n   112→- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n   113→  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n   114→  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n   115→  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n   116→\n   117→- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n   118→  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n   119→  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n   120→    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n   121→    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n   122→    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n   123→  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n   124→  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n   125→  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n   126→\n   127→- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n   128→  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n   129→  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n   130→  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n   131→  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n   132→  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n   133→\n   134→- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n   135→- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n   136→  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n   137→\n   138→- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n   139→- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n   140→\n   141→- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n   142→  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n   143→\n   144→- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n   145→  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n   146→- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n   147→  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n   148→\n   149→- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n   150→  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n   151→  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n   152→  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n   153→  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n   154→  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n   155→\n   156→## Python environment\n   157→\n   158→- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n   159→- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n   160→- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n   161→\n   162→## Decoder Reference\n   163→\n   164→- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n   165→- Optional arguments:\n   166→  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n   167→  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n   168→  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n   169→- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n   170→\n   171→### Purpose\n   172→\n   173→These functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n   174→1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   175→2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   176→3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   177→4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n   178→5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n   179→\n   180→### Consistency requirements\n   181→\n   182→Your processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n   183→- Loading of data\n   184→- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n   185→- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n   186→- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n   187→\n   188→To check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \n   189→\n   190→Whenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \n   191→\n   192→Discrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above. \n   193→\n   194→Carefully verify your work after every step. Variables important to experiments\n   278→  - Temporal alignment\n   279→  - Temporal binning\n   280→- Read the reference paper to find information about expected decoder accuracy.\n   281→\n   282→**Done when**: You have documented expected dataset statistics and processing details in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 3\".\n   283→\n   284→**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 4, update Step 3 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   285→\n   286→---\n   287→\n   288→### Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   289→\n   290→**Goal**: Verify your understanding is consistent across all sources. \n   291→\n   292→**Actions**:\n   293→- Compare your understanding of:\n   294→  - Important functions from exploring the reference code\n   295→  - Dataset structure from exploring the dataset\n   296→  - Analysis described in the reference texts\n   297→- Note and investigate any discrepancies\n   298→- In particular, look for discrepancies in:\n   299→  - Experimental and behavioral variables\n   300→  - Data processing\n   301→  - Data curation\n   302→- Iterate until your understanding of the reference code, dataset, and reference texts are consistent\n   303→\n   304→**Done when**: You have resolved all discrepancies and documented your final understanding in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 4\".\n   305→\n   306→**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 5, update Step 4 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   307→\n   308→---\n   309→\n   310→### Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   311→\n   312→**Goal**: Create a detailed plan for mapping source data to target format.\n   313→\n   314→**Actions**:\n   315→- Map source data elements to target format components:\n   316→  - Which variables contain neural activity?\n   317→  - Identify ALL available experimental variables\n   318→  - Which variables should be decoder **inputs** vs **outputs**? (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   319→  - How to discretize continuous outputs (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   320→  - Which inputs/outputs should be **time-varying** vs **per-trial** (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   321→  - How does the reference code load and manipulate this data?\n   322→  - When possible, import or copy code from the reference code in `code` directory. \n   323→- Consult CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4 and reference materials to determine:\n   324→  - Temporal binning for spike rates (if applicable)\n   325→  - Temporal alignment of trials\n   326→  - Data filtering and curation rules for neurons, trials, and sessions\n   327→  - Brain regions recorded from\n   328→- Mapping should be consistent with CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4.\n   329→- Check for variables indicating valid data periods — exclude invalid data\n   330→- Note all decisions, edge cases, and data quality issues\n   331→- Plan and document **sanity and consistency checks** based on dataset size information from the reference texts, code, and data\n   332→\n   333→**Done when**:\n   334→- You have a complete mapping document in CONVERSION_NOTES.md specifying every source-to-target variable mapping under \"Step 5\"\n   335→- You have documented all decisions under \"Step 5\"\n   336→- You have documented sanity and consistency checks under \"Step 5\"\n   337→\n   338→**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 6, update Step 5 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   339→\n   340→---\n   341→\n   342→### Step 6: Script Development\n   343→\n   344→**Goal**: Write the conversion script to `convert_data.py`.\n   345→\n   346→**Actions**:\n   347→- Write all conversion code in the file `convert_data.py`\n   348→- Script should run as **`python -u convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`**\n   349→- Include options:\n   350→  - `--full` (default): Process all sessions\n   351→  - `--sample`: Process only 2 sessions for testing\n   352→  - `--show-processing`: Plot visualizations of EVERY processing step for up to 2 sessions. Save plots as `processing_<session_id>.png`.\n   353→- Reuse code from reference code bases where appropriate\n   354→- Write efficient code:\n   355→  - Vectorize loops\n   356→  - Avoid unnecessary file I/O\n   357→  - Use parallel processing if beneficial\n   358→  - Print timing information to find bottlenecks\n   359→- Plots for `--show-processing` mode should visually convince the user that every step of the conversion is correct.\n   360→  - There are no temporal misalignments\n   361→  - Discretization of continuous outputs is correct\n   362→- Write clear, well-documented functions\n   363→- Handle missing data appropriately (consult references, use sensible defaults, document)\n   364→- Validate data shapes and types at each step\n   365→- Include sanity checks (e.g., trial counts match across arrays)\n   366→\n   367→**Done when**: `convert_data.py` exists and runs efficiently without errors.\n   368→\n   369→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 7 until `convert_data.py` exists and you have updated Step 6 in CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   370→\n   371→---\n   372→\n   373→### Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n   374→\n   375→**Goal**: Validate the conversion on a small sample before processing the full dataset.\n   376→\n   377→**Actions**:\n   378→1. Run `python -u convert_data.py sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing 2>&1 | tee conversion_sample_out.txt`\n   379→  a. Verify the sample data structure matches the specification.\n   380→  b. Check dimensions are consistent across trials/sessions.\n   381→  c. Check that no data is missed during conversion. \n   382→  d. Validate that metadata accurately describes the data.\n   383→2. Improve efficiency of conversion.\n   384→  a. Look at and note timing information for the conversion. \n   385→  b. Estimate how long the full conversion will run. When you do this, make sure that your estimate accounts for differences in lengths/numbers of trials/sessions. \n   386→  c. If full conversion time estimate is longer than 15 minutes, speed up the code by writing more efficient code (vectorizing loops, removing unnecessary or redundant computations) and/or including parallel processing. \n   387→3. Run `python -u train_decoder.py sample_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_sample_out.txt`.\n   388→4. Analyze the output file `verification_sample_out.txt`:\n   389→  a. Verify no errors reported.\n   390→  b. Attempt to address any warnings\n   391→  c. Check input ranges and output value distributions against expectations from reference texts.\n   392→\n   393→**Done when**: \n   394→- `sample_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n   395→- `verification_sample_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n   396→- Processing plots show no anomalies\n   397→- Document sample statistics in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 7\"\n   398→\n   399→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 8 until `sample_data.pkl` and `verification_sample_out.txt` files exist, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 7 is filled in. \n   400→\n   401→---\n   402→\n   403→### Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n   404→\n   405→**Goal**: Verify the decoder runs successfully on sample data.\n   406→\n   407→**Actions**:\n   408→1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py sample_data.pkl 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_sample_out.txt`\n   409→2. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n   410→3. **Check accuracy**: Verify accuracy is above chance for EVERY output\n   411→\n   412→**Investigate any issues**:\n   413→- If accuracy is low: check for conversion bugs or reconsider output representation\n   414→\n   415→**Done when**: \n   416→- Decoder training completes without errors\n   417→- Loss decreases over epochs\n   418→- Accuracies are above chance\n   419→- Document all results in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 8\"\n   420→\n   421→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 9 until `train_decoder_sample_out.txt` file exists, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 8 is filled in. \n   422→\n   423→---\n   424→\n   425→### Step 9: Full Conversion and Validation\n   426→\n   427→**Goal**: Process the complete dataset.\n   428→\n   429→**Actions**:\n   430→1. Review your time estimate from Step 7.\n   431→2. If estimated time is long (e.g. longer than 15 minutes), optimize bottlenecks first.\n   432→3. Run `python -u convert_data.py converted_data.pkl --full 2>&1 | tee conversion_full_out.txt`\n   433→4. As the code runs, update your estimates of how long processing will take. If it is much longer than your previous estimate (> 1.5x), kill the process, optimize bottlenecks, and repeat. \n   434→5. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_full_out.txt`. \n   435→6. Check that no data was lost during conversion \n   436→7. Spot-check a few sessions to verify data integrity\n   437→8. **Check for consistency** between dataset statistics in `verification_full_out.txt` and the reference texts\n   438→9. Investigate any inconsistencies, and revise the conversion script until all dataset statistics are consistent\n   439→\n   440→**Done when**:\n   441→- `converted_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n   442→- `verification_full_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n   443→- **ALL** dataset statistics are consistent with values from reference texts\n   444→- You have documented statistics and consistency in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 9\"\n   445→\n   446→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 10 until `converted_data.pkl` and `verification_full_out.txt` files exist and CONVERSION_NOTES.MD Step 9 is filled in.\n   447→\n   448→---\n   449→\n   450→### Step 10: Critical Review 1\n   451→\n   452→**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain.\n   453→\n   454→**Check 1: Output log verification**\n   455→Read through `verification_full_out.txt` to find and fix **all errors**. Address each warning by either fixing its cause or verifying it that it is not possible to fix it. Explain why each warning could not be fixed in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   456→\n   457→**Check 2: Construct sanity checks**\n   458→- Develop at least one sanity check each on neural, input, and output data.\n   459→- These must involve loading in the original data files (NOT through your conversion code).\n   460→- Construct comparison tests and criteria using `np.allclose()`. \n   461→- These tests can be spot-checks of the data, e.g. neural activity at trial 5, timepoint 10, neuron 3. \n   462→- Fix all mismatches/sanity check failures. \n   463→- Document your sanity checks to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   464→\n   465→**Check 3: Reference code comparison**\n   466→- Compare your code to the reference code and methods. \n   467→- For each major processing step — (a) data loading, (b) neuron/trial filtering, (c) temporal alignment, (d) binning, (e) input construction, (f) output construction — identify the corresponding code in your script and in the reference, and compare the logic. \n   468→- Check whether you use the same variables, filtering, and processing as the reference code. \n   469→- Fix any mismatches. \n   470→- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   471→- Explain your reasoning for any differences in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`.\n   472→\n   473→**Check 4: Key statistics comparison**\n   474→- Verify that key statistics (number of animals, sessions, trials, neurons, input and output ranges, output distributions) match information available in the reference papers. \n   475→- Write code to investigate any discrepancies. \n   476→- Fix any discovered issues. \n   477→- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   478→\n   479→**Check 5: Check for edge cases**\n   480→- Check for off-by-one errors at the beginning/ends of trials, sessions, and subjects, or in definitions of the inputs and outputs. \n   481→- There may be minor issues in the data that your code must handle. Check that your code robustly handles issues in the original data. \n   482→\n   483→**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n   484→1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n   485→2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n   486→3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n   487→4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n   488→\n   489→Write a report to CONVERSION_NOTES.md describing **every** check you did, to help convince the user that the conversion code works. **Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 10\".\n   490→\n   491→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 11 until you have checked for consistency between **every** statistic in the reference texts, code, and data and documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Step 10. \n   492→\n   493→---\n   494→\n   495→### Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n   496→\n   497→**Goal**: Validate the decoder on the **complete** dataset.\n   498→\n   499→**⚠️ IMPORTANT**: This step must complete fully. Do not skip or abbreviate. Do not proceed to Step 12 until this step is completely finished.\n   500→\n   501→**Actions**:\n   502→1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --plot-samples 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_full_out.txt`. If the GPU does not have sufficient RAM for the network training, use the flag `--cpu` to specify to use the CPU to train. \n   503→2. **Wait for complete execution** — this may take significant time for large datasets\n   504→3. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n   505→4. **Check accuracy**: High accuracy is an indicator that data conversion has been done correctly. Accuracy near chance is a sign that there might be issues in temporal alignment of signals, choice of data streams, or filtering or processing of data. Compare accuracy to expectations based on the papers. \n   506→\n   507→**Done when**: \n   508→1. Full decoder training completes (the script finishes running)\n   509→2. Accuracy results are documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 11\" with a table of accuracies\n   510→\n   511→---\n   512→\n   513→### Step 12: Critical Review 2\n   514→\n   515→**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain. Perform all of the following checks:\n   516→\n   517→**Check 1: Accuracy vs chance analysis**\n   518→- For each output, compare validation accuracy to chance (1/num_classes)\n   519→- Accuracy below chance indicates a bug, investigate and fix conversion issues to improve this. \n   520→- Accuracy below 1.5x chance may also indicate a bug, investigate whether changes in conversion logic could improve accuracy. \n   521→\n   522→**Check 2: Accuracy comparison to papers**\n   523→- Find every decoding accuracy reported in the reference papers\n   524→- Create a table comparing your accuracy to the papers's accuracy for each variable\n   525→- If your accuracy is lower than the reported accuracy, this may indicate a bug in your conversion that you must fix. \n   526→- Differences in algorithm/architecture should be relatively small because of the data dimensionality and size. Do not use this as an excuse to dismiss differences, investigate them completely before moving on. \n   527→\n   528→**Check 3: Train vs validation gap**\n   529→- If training accuracy is much higher (>1.5x) than validation accuracy for any output, investigate overfitting or data leakage. \n   530→\n   531→**If accuracy is low for an output, try these specific debugging steps:**\n   532→1. Verify the output values are correct by loading raw data and checking 3 specific trials\n   533→2. Check temporal alignment — plot neural + output for a single trial to verify they're synchronized\n   534→3. Check whether the output has enough variation (not 99% one class)\n   535→4. Check that filtering steps for neural activity streams are followed. \n   536→5. Check that processing of the data matches the reference code and papers. \n   537→\n   538→**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n   539→1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n   540→2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n   541→3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n   542→4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n   543→\n   544→**Report all checks and results** to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` to convince the user that the conversion and decoding has been done correctly. \n   545→\n   546→**Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 12\".\n   547→\n   548→---\n   549→\n   550→### Step 13: Documentation and Cleanup\n   551→\n   552→**Goal**: Finalize documentation and organize files.\n   553→\n   554→**Actions**:\n   555→- Ensure CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete with all:\n   556→  - Key decisions and rationale\n   557→  - Checks for correctness of the conversion\n   558→  - Validation results and tables\n   559→  - Any issues found and how they were resolved\n   560→- Create a user-facing `README.md` summarizing:\n   561→  - Dataset description\n   562→  - How to load and use the converted data\n   563→  - Output format specification\n   564→  - Key statistics\n   565→- Move analysis/investigation scripts to a `cache/` folder\n   566→- Create `cache/README_CACHE.md` documenting cached files\n   567→\n   568→**Done when**: README.md exists, CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete, and directory is clean.\n   569→\n   570→---\n   571→\n   572→## CONVERSION_NOTES.md Template\n   573→\n   574→**Create this file IMMEDIATELY in Step 0. Do this BEFORE exploring any code or data. Document everything in this file as you work.\n\n2. **FOLLOW STEPS IN ORDER**: Do not skip steps. Do not proceed to step N+1 until step N is complete. Each step has \"Done when\" criteria — verify them before moving on.\n\n3. **MATCH THE REFERENCE PROCESSING**: Your processing must match what's described in the reference papers and code. You will be assessed on this consistency.\n\n---\n\n## Project Context\n\n- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference papers and code repository. \n- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n- We will assess your correctness on **matching the loading and processing described in the reference texts and code**. \n\n## Reference Information\n\n- The reference **papers**:\n  - \"A brain-wide map of neural activity during complex behaviour\" describes how the data were collected and is in the file `datapaper.pdf`.\n  - \"Exploiting correlations across trials and behavioral sessions to improve neural decoding\" describes how the code were used and is in the file `methodpaper.pdf`.\n  - A white paper about the data architecture is in `dataarchitecture.pdf\n- Some parts of the papers that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `methods.txt`. \n- **Code** from the methods paper are in the directory `code`.\n- **Data** from the data paper are in the directory `data`. Some data may be missing and may need to be downloaded. \n- **Documentation** about how to access the data can be found at https://docs.internationalbrainlab.org/public_docs/public_introduction.html.\n\n## Decoder Task\n\nTemporally align based on stimulus onset\n\n### Decoder Inputs:\n- Time since stimulus onset, continuous, time-varying\n- Trial number in block, continuous, per-trial\n\n### Decoder Outputs\n- Choice, binary, per-trial, left = 0, right = 1\n- Prior probability of left, per-trial, 0.2 -> 0, 0.5 -> 1, 0.8 -> 2\n- Wheel speed discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\n- Whisker motion energy discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\n\n## Target Data Format\n\n- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n\n```python\ndata = {\n    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'metadata': {\n        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        ...\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Key Format Requirements\n\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n\n## Python environment\n\n- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n\n## Decoder Reference\n\n- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n- Optional arguments:\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n\n### Purpose\n\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n\n### Consistency requirements\n\nYour processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n- Loading of data\n- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n\nTo check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \n\nWhenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \n\nDiscrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above. \n\nCarefully verify your work after every step. np.ndarray,\n   369→    target_values: np.ndarray,\n   370→    align_times: np.ndarray,\n   371→    window: tuple[float, float] = TIME_WINDOW,\n   372→    binsize: float = BINSIZE_S,\n   373→) -> tuple[list[np.ndarray | None], np.ndarray]:\n   374→    valid_source = np.isfinite(target_times) & np.isfinite(target_values)\n   375→    target_times = target_times[valid_source]\n   376→    target_values = target_values[valid_source]\n   377→    interval_len = window[1] - window[0]\n   378→    n_bins = int(np.ceil(interval_len / binsize))\n   379→    x_rel = np.linspace(window[0] + binsize, window[1], n_bins)\n   380→    outputs: list[np.ndarray | None] = []\n   381→    mask = np.zeros(len(align_times), dtype=bool)\n   382→\n   383→    idx_beg = np.searchsorted(target_times, align_times + window[0], side=\"right\")\n   384→    idx_end = np.searchsorted(target_times, align_times + window[1], side=\"left\")\n   385→    for i, align_time in enumerate(align_times):\n   386→        start = align_time + window[0]\n   387→        end = align_time + window[1]\n   388→        vals = target_values[idx_beg[i] : idx_end[i]]\n   389→        ts = target_times[idx_beg[i] : idx_end[i]]\n   390→        if len(vals) == 0:\n   391→            outputs.append(None)\n   392→            continue\n   393→        if np.abs(start - ts[0]) > binsize or np.abs(end - ts[-1]) > binsize:\n   394→            outputs.append(None)\n   395→            continue\n   396→        interp = interp1d(ts, vals, kind=\"linear\", fill_value=\"extrapolate\")\n   397→        outputs.append(interp(align_time + x_rel).astype(np.float32))\n   398→        mask[i] = True\n   399→    return outputs, mask\n   400→\n   401→\n   402→def map_choice_to_binary(choice_values: right\n   406→    if np.any(mapped < 0):\n   407→        raise ValueError(\"Unexpected choice values after masking\")\n   408→    return mapped\n   409→\n   410→\n   411→def map_prior_to_categorical(prob_left: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n   412→    mapped = np.full(prob_left.shape, -1, dtype=np.int16)\n   413→    mapped[np.isclose(prob_left, 0.2)] = 0\n   414→    mapped[np.isclose(prob_left, 0.5)] = 1\n   415→    mapped[np.isclose(prob_left, 0.8)] = 2\n   416→    if np.any(mapped < 0):\n   417→        vals = np.unique(prob_left[mapped < 0])\n   418→ [np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).ravel() for v in values if len(v)]\n   428→    if not flat_values:\n   429→        raise ValueError(\"Cannot compute tertile edges from empty values\")\n   430→    concat = np.concatenate(flat_values)\n   431→    q1, q2 = np.quantile(concat, [1 / 3, 2 / 3])\n   432→    if not np.isfinite(q1) or not np.isfinite(q2) or q1 >= q2:\n   433→        mn = float(np.min(concat))\n   434→        mx = float(np.max(concat))\n   435→        if mn == mx:\n   436→            eps = np.finfo(np.float32).eps\n   437→            return mn + eps, mn + 2 * eps\n   438→        step = (mx - mn) / 3.0\n   439→        return mn + step, mn + 2.0 * step\n   440→    return float(q1), float(q2)\n   441→\n   442→\n   443→def discretize_three_bins(values: np.ndarray, load_session_spikes(\n    session_path: Path,\n    br: BrainRegions,\n    label_threshold: int = GOOD_CLUSTER_LABEL,\n) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray, int, int]:\n    probe_paths = np.ndarray,\n    window: tuple[float, float] = TIME_WINDOW,\n    binsize: float = BINSIZE_S,\n) -> list[np.ndarray]:\n    interval_len = window[1] - window[0]\n    n_bins = int(np.ceil(interval_len / spike_times[idx0:idx1],\n                spike_clusters[idx0:idx1],\n                xbin=binsize,\n                xlim=[start, end],\n            )\n            if counts.size:\n load_wheel_speed(session_path: Path) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    wheel_pos_file = pick_one_file(session_path / \"alf\", \"_ibl_wheel.position.npy\")\n    wheel_ts_file = {session_path}\")\n    pos = np.asarray(np.load(wheel_pos_file), dtype=np.float64)\n    ts = np.asarray(np.load(wheel_ts_file), dtype=np.float64)\n    if ts.ndim == 2 and ts.shape[1] == 2:\n        ts load_whisker_motion_energy(session_path: Path) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, str]:\n    for camera in (\"left\", \"right\"):\n        me_file = pick_one_file(session_path / \"alf\", continue\n        values = np.asarray(np.load(me_file), dtype=np.float64)\n        times = np.asarray(np.load(times_file), dtype=np.float64)\n        if len(values) != len(times):\n            n = min(len(values), len(times))\n            values = values[:n]\n            times = times[:n]\n        return times, values, camera\n    raise FileNotFoundError(f\"Missing whisker motion energy files for {session_path}\")\n\n\ndef interpolate_behavior_trials(\n    target_times: np.ndarray,\n    target_values: np.ndarray,\n    align_times: np.ndarray,\n    window: tuple[float, float] = TIME_WINDOW,\n    binsize: float = BINSIZE_S,\n) -> tuple[list[np.ndarray | None], np.ndarray]:\n    valid_source = np.isfinite(target_times) & np.isfinite(target_values)\n    target_times = target_times[valid_source]\n    target_values = target_values[valid_source]\n    interval_len = window[1] - window[0]\n    n_bins = int(np.ceil(interval_len / binsize))\n    x_rel = np.linspace(window[0] + binsize, window[1], n_bins)\n    outputs: list[np.ndarray | None] = []\n    mask = np.zeros(len(align_times), dtype=bool)\n\n    idx_beg = np.searchsorted(target_times, align_times + window[0], side=\"right\")\n    idx_end = np.searchsorted(target_times, align_times + window[1], side=\"left\")\n    for i, align_time in enumerate(align_times):\n        start = align_time + window[0]\n        end = align_time + window[1]\n        vals = target_values[idx_beg[i] : idx_end[i]]\n        ts = target_times[idx_beg[i] : idx_end[i]]\n        if len(vals) == 0:\n            outputs.append(None)\n            continue\n        if np.abs(start - ts[0]) > binsize or np.abs(end - ts[-1]) > binsize:\n            outputs.append(None)\n            continue\n        interp = interp1d(ts, vals, kind=\"linear\", fill_value=\"extrapolate\")\n        outputs.append(interp(align_time + x_rel).astype(np.float32))\n        mask[i] = True\n    return outputs, mask\n\n\ndef map_choice_to_binary(choice_values: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    mapped = np.full(choice_values.shape, -1, dtype=np.int16)\n    mapped[choice_values == 1] = 0   # left\n    mapped[choice_values == -1] = 1  # right\n    if np.any(mapped < 0):\n        raise ValueError(\"Unexpected choice values after masking\")\n    return mapped\n\n\ndef map_prior_to_categorical(prob_left: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    mapped = np.full(prob_left.shape, -1, dtype=np.int16)\n    mapped[np.isclose(prob_left, 0.2)] = 0\n    mapped[np.isclose(prob_left, 0.5)] = 1\n    mapped[np.isclose(prob_left, 0.8)] = 2\n    if np.any(mapped < 0):\n        vals = np.unique(prob_left[mapped < 0])\n        raise ValueError(f\"Unexpected probabilityLeft values: {vals}\")\n    return mapped\n\n\ndef make_time_input() -> np.ndarray:\n    return np.linspace(TIME_WINDOW[0] + BINSIZE_S, TIME_WINDOW[1], N_BINS, dtype=np.float32)\n\n\ndef compute_tertile_edges(values: Iterable[np.ndarray]) -> tuple[float, float]:\n    flat_values = [np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).ravel() for v in values if len(v)]\n    if not flat_values:\n        raise ValueError(\"Cannot compute tertile edges from empty values\")\n    concat = np.concatenate(flat_values)\n    q1, q2 = np.quantile(concat, [1 / 3, 2 / 3])\n    if not np.isfinite(q1) or not np.isfinite(q2) or q1 >= q2:\n        mn = float(np.min(concat))\n        mx = float(np.max(concat))\n        if mn == mx:\n            eps = np.finfo(np.float32).eps\n            return mn + eps, mn + 2 * eps\n        step = (mx - mn) / 3.0\n        return mn + step, mn + 2.0 * step\n    return float(q1), float(q2)\n\n\ndef discretize_three_bins(values: np.ndarray, edges: tuple[float, float]) -> np.ndarray:\n    return np.digitize(values, bins=np.array(edges, dtype=np.float32), right=False).astype(np.int16)\n\n\ndef format_seconds(seconds: float) -> str:\n    if seconds < 60:\n        return f\"{seconds:.1f}s\"\n    minutes, sec = divmod(seconds, 60)\n    return f\"{int(minutes)}m {sec:.1f}s\"\n\n\ndef process_session(spec: SessionSpec, br: BrainRegions) -> ProcessedSession | None:\n    t0 = time.perf_counter()\n    trials = load_trials_table(spec.session_path)\n    raw_n_trials = len(trials)\n    trial_mask = compute_trial_mask(trials)\n    block_trial_number = spec.session_path, br\n    )\n    if n_clusters_good == 0:\n        print(f\"[skip] {spec.eid}: no good clusters after QC\")\n        return None\n\n    try:\n        wheel_times, wheel_speed = {spec.eid}: {exc}\")\n        return None\n\n    masked_trials = trials.loc[trial_mask].reset_index(drop=False)\n    if len(masked_trials) < 2:\n        print(f\"[skip] {spec.eid}: fewer than 2 trials after trial mask\")\n        return None\n\n    align_times = masked_trials[\"stimOn_times\"].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64)\n    neural_trials = bin_spikes_for_trials(\n        spike_times,\n        spike_clusters,\n        n_clusters=n_clusters_good,\n        align_times=align_times,\n    )\n    wheel_trials, wheel_mask = interpolate_behavior_trials(wheel_times, wheel_speed, align_times)\n    whisk_trials, whisk_mask = interpolate_behavior_trials(whisk_times, whisk_values, align_times)\n    neural_mask = np.array([np.any(trial) for trial in neural_trials], dtype=bool)\n    combined_mask = wheel_mask & whisk_mask & neural_mask\n\n    if combined_mask.sum() < 2:\n        print(f\"[skip] {spec.eid}: fewer than 2 trials after behavior/neural alignment\")\n        return None\n\n    neural_keep = [neural_trials[i] for i, keep in enumerate(combined_mask) if keep]\n    wheel_keep = [wheel_trials[i] for i, keep in enumerate(combined_mask) if keep]\n    whisk_keep = [whisk_trials[i] for i, keep in enumerate(combined_mask) if keep]\n    masked_keep = masked_trials.loc[combined_mask].reset_index(drop=True)\n\n    time_input = make_time_input()\n    inputs = []\n    choice = []\n    prior = []\n    choice_vals = map_choice_to_binary(masked_keep[\"choice\"].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64))\n    prior_vals = map_prior_to_categorical(masked_keep[\"probabilityLeft\"].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64))\n    block_vals = block_trial_number[masked_keep[\"index\"].to_numpy()]\n\n    for block_num, choice_val, prior.append(np.full(N_BINS, prior_val, dtype=np.int16))\n\n    elapsed = time.perf_counter() - t0\n    print(\n        f\"[ok] {spec.eid}: raw_trials={raw_n_trials}, kept_trials={len(neural_keep)}, \"\n        f\"good_clusters={n_clusters_good}, whisker={whisk_source}, elapsed={format_seconds(elapsed)}\"\n    )\n    return ProcessedSession(\n        eid=spec.eid,\n        subject=spec.subject,\n        neural=neural_keep,\n        inputs=inputs,\n        choice=choice,\n        prior=prior,\n        wheel_cont=[np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float32) for x in wheel_keep],\n        whisker_cont=[np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float32) for x in whisk_keep],\n        cluster_regions=np.asarray(cluster_regions, dtype=object),\n  169→\n   170→**Neuron curation rules**:\n   171→- Data paper main analyses exclude units failing single-unit QC metrics: amplitude > 50 µV, noise cutoff < 20 µV, and refractory-period-violation all spike-sorted clusters, then stores a `good_clusters` label in metadata; explicit neuron filtering is not applied in `prepare_data()`.\n   174→\n   175→**Trial curation rules**:\n   176→- Dynamic-behavior performance reported with `R2`; extracted Figure 8 text shows example IBL unaligned values up to roughly `0.55` and other examples up to `0.75` depending on task / model |\n   186→| Whisker motion energy | Dynamic-behavior performance reported with `R2`; extracted Figure 8 text shows example IBL unaligned values up to roughly `0.80` |\n   187→\n   188→---\n   189→\n   190→## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   191→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   192→\n   193→### Discrepancies Found\n   194→| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n   195→|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n   196→| Session universe | `bwm_release.csv` contains 459 release sessions / 699 probe insertions; method repo says decoders use 433 IBL sessions | Local cache has 461 concrete session directories, 441 with wheel + spikes + either whisker, 434 with left whisker specifically | Data paper release: 459 sessions; method paper: 433 sessions | Treat 459 sessions in `bwm_release.csv` as the canonical release index. The method-paper 433-session set is a downstream usable subset after additional availability / preprocessing constraints; I will derive usable sessions from actual preprocessing rather than force 433 a priori. |\n   197→| Alignment / time window | `0_data_caching.py` uses `align_time='stimOn_times'`, `time_window=(-0.5, 1.5)`, `binsize=0.02` for all cached behaviors | Raw data support stim-on and first-movement events | Method paper prose says choice uses stim onset, prior uses a pre-stimulus window, and wheel / whisker use first-movement alignment | For this conversion, use the executable repository settings of 2 s windows, 20 ms bins, and stimulus-onset alignment, because the user explicitly requires “Temporally align based on stimulus onset” and the reference code implements this directly. |\n   198→| Neural bin size | Reference code bins all trial-aligned data at 20 ms | Raw data are continuous spike times | Method paper has internal inconsistency: generic model description says 20 ms, but data-processing prose states 50 ms bins for choice / prior | Use 20 ms bins. This matches the executable code and the top-level model description (`T = 100` over 2 s). |\n   199→| Neuron filtering | `prepare_data()` loads all spike-sorted clusters; metadata stores `good_clusters = label >= 1` | Raw cluster metrics contain QC fields and labels; the full local release has 75,708 clusters with `label >= 1` | Data paper main analyses restrict to well-isolated neurons; method paper text says “all neurons, sorted by Kilosort 2.5” | For this export, keep `clusters.metrics.label >= 1`. This matches the data paper’s well-isolated-neuron count exactly in the 459-session local release and keeps the dense pickle tractable. |\n   200→| Trial filtering | `load_trials_and_mask()` excludes missing key events, RT outside `[0.08, 2.0]`, `choice == 0`, and `prepare_data()` also sets `max_trial_len=10.0` | Raw trial tables contain the needed event columns | Data paper excludes missing events and RT outside `[0.08, 2.0]`; no-choice exclusion is not emphasized there | Use the full reference-code trial mask, since it contains the paper overlap plus extra curation actually used by the decoder repository. |\n   201→| Whisker stream source | `bin_behaviors()` loads left whisker motion energy first, falls back to right if left is unavailable | Local cache has 434 left, 421 right, 441 either | Papers describe whisker motion energy generally, without fixing left vs right in the decoder section | Use left whisker motion energy when present, otherwise right, matching the reference code exactly. |\n   202→| Choice coding | Code reads `trials.choice` directly | Raw data encode choices as `-1`, `+1`, and `0` for no-go | Decoder task requires left = 0, right = 1 | After applying the no-choice mask, map the two remaining raw values to `{0, 1}` in the target export. |\n   203→| Prior / block coding | Code uses `trials.probabilityLeft` as `block` | Raw values are `0.2`, `0.5`, `0.8` | Papers describe unbiased 0.5 block then biased 0.2/0.8 blocks | Export prior as the user-required categorical mapping `0.2 -> 0`, `0.5 -> 1`, `0.8 -> 2`. |\n   204→| Dynamic-behavior decoding target construction | Zhang code caches wheel / whisker as interpolated time-varying traces on the common 20 ms trial grid | Raw wheel / motion-energy arrays are continuous streams with their own sampling | Data paper’s own wheel decoder description uses a causal window around movement and regression `R2` | For this project, dynamic outputs will be discretized after interpolation onto the shared stimulus-onset grid, because the target format requires categorical outputs and the user requested a single common alignment event. |\n   205→\n   206→### Final Understanding\n   207→- The most defensible reference for the conversion mechanics is the Zhang 2025 repository’s preprocessing code, not every detail of the prose papers, because the papers and code are not fully internally consistent.\n   208→- The data paper supplies the global dataset facts and core curation rules: 139 mice, 459 release sessions, 699 insertions, 621,733 units, RT and missing-event exclusions, and the definition of well-isolated neurons.\n   209→- The Zhang repository supplies the concrete session-processing pipeline used for decoder-ready data:\n   210→  - merge probes within session\n   211→  - load spike-sorted clusters and expose QC metadata\n   212→  - mask trials with missing critical events / out-of-range RT / no-choice / long trial\n   213→  - interpolate behavior streams onto a fixed 20 ms grid\n   214→  - create 2 s trial windows aligned to `stimOn_times`\n   215→- The user’s decoder task explicitly overrides any remaining ambiguity by requiring stimulus-onset alignment and categorical outputs for all decoded variables.\n   216→- Therefore the conversion plan for later steps will use:\n   217→  - session-level units of analysis\n   218→  - merged probes per session\n   219→  - `clusters.metrics.label >= 1` as the neuron inclusion rule\n   220→  - 20 ms bins over `[-0.5, 1.5]` s relative to stimulus onset\n   221→  - trial masking based on the Zhang code\n   222→  - left-then-right whisker fallback\n   223→  - categorical remapping for choice / prior and discretization for wheel speed / whisker motion energy\n   224→\n   225→---\n   226→\n   227→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   228→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   229→\n   230→### Variable Mapping\n   231→| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n   232→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   233→| `spikes.times`, `spikes.clusters` from all probes in one session | `neural` | Merge probes, keep clusters with `clusters.metrics.label >= 1`, bin counts into 20 ms bins over `[-0.5, 1.5]` s relative to `stimOn_times`; store each trial as `(n_neurons, 100)` float array | `load_spiking_data`, `merge_probes`, `bin_spiking_data` | Neural values will be raw binned spike counts, not standardized z-scores |\n   234→| common trial time grid | `input[0]` | Signed time since stimulus onset at each bin, repeated for every trial as a length-100 vector | Code uses `binsize=0.02`, `time_window=(-0.5, 1.5)`; behavior interpolation uses right-edge sample times | Planned values: `[-0.48, -0.46, ..., 1.50]` s to match behavior interpolation grid |\n   235→| raw `probabilityLeft` block sequence | `input[1]` | Trial number within current probability block, computed on the original trial sequence, then repeated across all 100 bins in the kept trial | Derived from trial table; no direct helper in repo | Block counter resets whenever `probabilityLeft` changes |\n   236→| raw `choice` | `output[0]` | Map raw IBL code to categorical side: `choice == 1 -> left -> 0`, `choice == -1 -> right -> 1`; repeat across all 100 bins | `bin_behaviors` loads `choice`; IBL docs / local examples define sign convention | Trials with `choice == 0` are excluded by reference mask |\n   237→| raw `probabilityLeft` | `output[1]` | Map `{0.2, 0.5, 0.8}` to `{0, 1, 2}` and repeat across all 100 bins | `bin_behaviors` loads this as `block` | User requested this categorical coding explicitly |\n   238→| wheel stream | `output[2]` | Load wheel velocity, take absolute value to obtain speed, interpolate onto stimulus-onset grid, then discretize into 3 global bins | `load_target_behavior('wheel-speed')`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | Bins will be dataset-wide tertiles over valid aligned samples |\n   239→| whisker motion energy stream | `output[3]` | Load left whisker motion energy; if unavailable, fall back to right; interpolate onto stimulus-onset grid; discretize into 3 global bins | `load_target_behavior('left-whisker-motion-energy' / 'right-whisker-motion-energy')`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | Bins will be dataset-wide tertiles over valid aligned samples |\n   240→| session subject metadata | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | Build unique subject list from kept sessions and index each session into it | `bwm_release.csv` / session path metadata | Deterministic session order required |\n   241→| cluster acronyms | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | Build global vocabulary of recorded acronyms and index each neuron | `clusters['acronym']` via `prepare_data` metadata | Use raw acronyms rather than Beryl remapping, because session export is neuron-level |\n   242→| session / conversion settings | `metadata` | Store task description, 20 ms bin size, `stimOn_times` alignment, offsets, discretization edges, release/source notes | Project-defined | Include enough fields to reproduce the export |\n   243→\n   244→### Key Decisions\n   245→1. **Use 2D time-varying arrays for both `input` and `output`**: Static trial variables (`choice`, `prior`, `trial_number_in_block`) will be repeated across the 100-bin time axis so every trial has consistent `(d, T)` tensors.\n   246→2. **Use the Zhang repository’s 2 s, 20 ms, stimulus-onset-aligned grid**: This matches the executable code and the user’s explicit alignment instruction, despite prose discrepancies in the papers.\n   247→3. **Keep clusters with `label >= 1`**: This matches the data paper’s well-isolated-unit count exactly in the full local release (`75,708`) and keeps the dense export tractable while remaining anchored to an explicit QC field already present in the reference code.\n   248→4. **Apply the reference trial mask before export**: Required events present, RT in `[0.08, 2.0]` s, `choice != 0`, and trial duration ≤ 10 s.\n   249→5. **Require sessions to have all core streams needed by this task**: spikes, trials, wheel, and at least one whisker-motion-energy camera; afterwards, require at least 2 valid kept trials per session.\n   250→6. **Use left whisker motion energy first, then right as fallback**: This exactly matches `bin_behaviors()` and maximizes session retention without inventing a new rule.\n   251→7. **Discretize wheel speed and whisker motion energy using global tertiles**: Global edges preserve a common categorical meaning across sessions and should keep class balance better than fixed-width bins.\n   252→8. **Compute trial number in block on the original unfiltered trial table**: Excluded trials should not renumber the latent block progression.\n   253→9. **Map choice polarity using rewarded nonzero-contrast trials**: Local data confirm `choice == 1` corresponds to left and `choice == -1` to right, so export will use left=0, right=1.\n   254→10. **Preserve raw region acronyms in `brain_regions`**: This avoids collapsing neurons across atlas levels and keeps the export closest to the cluster metadata.\n   255→\n   256→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   257→- [ ] Neural sanity check: for a chosen session/trial/neuron/bin, compare exported spike count to a direct count from raw `spikes.times` / `spikes.clusters` within the corresponding raw bin boundaries using `np.allclose()`.\n   258→- [ ] Input sanity check: verify exported `time_since_stimulus_onset` exactly matches the constructed common grid and exported `trial_number_in_block` matches a direct raw-table block counter for selected trials.\n   259→- [ ] Output sanity check 1: verify exported choice / prior values for selected trials against raw `trials.choice` and `trials.probabilityLeft` using direct remapping.\n   260→- [ ] Output sanity check 2: verify aligned continuous wheel speed / whisker values at selected trial-bin locations against direct interpolation from raw streams before discretization, then verify discretized class assignments from stored global bin edges.\n   261→- [ ] Session-count sanity check: compare numbers of kept sessions, trials, and neurons against the release table, the local cache, and the paper-reported totals to explain every reduction.\n   262→\n   263→---\n   264→\n   265→## Step 6: Script No delta-F/F computation is involved.\n- Reference preprocessing aligns trials to `stimOn_times` with `time_window=(-0.5, 1.5)` seconds and `binsize=0.02` seconds, giving 2 s trial windows in 20 ms bins.\n- `prepare_data()` merges all probes from an `eid` into one session representation before decoding.\n- Trial curation in `load_trials_and_mask()` uses defaults `min_rt=0.08`, `max_rt=2.0`, excludes NaNs in `stimOn_times`, `choice`, `feedback_times`, `probabilityLeft`, `firstMovement_times`, `feedbackType`, excludes `choice == 0`, and in `prepare_data()` additionally enforces `max_trial_len=10.0`.\n- The Zhang 2025 caching script calls `load_spiking_data()` without `qc`, so it loads all labeled clusters, but metadata preserves a `good_clusters` flag computed as `clusters['label'] >= 1`.\n- Brain regions are taken from cluster acronyms and can be remapped to Beryl for region-level decoding; the caching script uses all regions together by default (`single_region=False`, `region='all'`).\n- For time-varying behavior, `get_behavior_per_interval()` interpolates each stream onto the aligned bin grid and rejects intervals that start too late, end too early, or contain disallowed NaNs.\n- `bin_behaviors()` constructs per-trial variables directly from the trials table:\n  - `choice` from `trials.choice`\n  - `block` from `trials.probabilityLeft`\n  - `reward` from `rewardVolume > 1`\n  - `contrast` from signed contrast difference\n- `align_spike_behavior()` is intended to drop invalid trials, but the boolean-mask code is weak (`target_mask and beh_mask` on Python lists). I need to treat the function as conceptual guidance and verify actual intended masking against data/text in later steps.\n- Downstream decoding treats `choice` as classification and wheel / whisker / pupil as regression in the Zhang code. Our target format differs because the user requires categorical outputs for all decoder outputs.\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n- `data/` paper:\n  - 90 unbiased trials at `probabilityLeft = 0.5`\n  - subsequent biased blocks alternate 0.2 / 0.8 left probability\n  - block lengths drawn from a truncated distribution between 20 and 100 trials\n- Data paper release / analysis curation:\n  - released sessions required at least 250 trials, good high-contrast performance, at least 3 incorrect trials, and hardware QC pass\n  - analyses in the paper retained sessions with at least 400 trials\n  - trials excluded if any of `choice`, `probabilityLeft`, `feedbackType`, `feedback_times`, `stimOn_times`, `firstMovement_times` were missing\n  - trials further excluded if `firstMovement_times - stimOn_times` was outside `[0.08, 2.00]` s\n  - well-isolated neurons required amplitude > 50 µV, noise cutoff < 20 µV, and refractory-period criterion\n- Method paper / decoder processing:\n  - all modeled trials are 2 s windows\n  - generic model description states 20 ms bins and `T = 100`\n  - choice uses stimulus-onset alignment with window `[-0.5, 1.5]` s\n  - prior uses stimulus-onset alignment with window `[-0.6, -0.1]` s in the text excerpt from `methods.txt`\n  - dynamic behaviors (wheel speed, whisker motion energy) use first-movement alignment, from movement onset to +1.0 s, with 20 ms bins\n  - the data paper’s own decoding section also describes wheel decoding as using bins from 200 ms before first movement to 1,000 ms after first movement, with a causal window of `W = 10` bins\n- Behavioral stream definitions:\n  - whisker motion energy is “the mean across pixels of the absolute value of the difference between adjacent frames” in a whisker-pad box\n  - wheel values include speed and velocity from the rotary encoder\n  - choice is binary\n  - prior / block is a three-state latent or discrete task variable derived from block probabilities\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n- Data paper main analyses exclude units failing single-unit QC metrics: amplitude > 50 µV, noise cutoff < 20 µV, and refractory-period-violation criterion.\n- Final region-level analyses further require at least 5 well-isolated neurons per session and at least two sessions for a region.\n- The Zhang 2025 code path itself loads all spike-sorted clusters, then stores a `good_clusters` label in metadata; explicit neuron filtering is not applied in `prepare_data()`.\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n- Missing-event trial exclusion: `choice`, `probabilityLeft`, `feedbackType`, `feedback_times`, `stimOn_times`, `firstMovement_times`\n- Reaction-time exclusion: keep only 0.08–2.00 s from `stimOn_times` to `firstMovement_times`\n- Data-paper release inclusion also requires session-level quality and performance thresholds\n- Zhang 2025 code additionally enforces `choice != 0` and `max_trial_len = 10.0` s via `load_trials_and_mask()`\n\n### Decoders Trained\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n| Choice | Method paper reports higher AUC than baseline; figure text exposes example values around `0.72`, `0.66`, `0.79` for compared models in Figure 4 / 5 context, but ordering is not completely recoverable from extracted text alone |\n| Prior | Method paper reports higher Pearson correlation than baseline; extracted figure text shows example values around `0.65`, `0.05`, `0.34` for compared models |\n| Wheel speed | Dynamic-behavior performance reported with `R2`; extracted Figure 8 text shows example IBL unaligned values up to roughly `0.55` and other examples up to `0.75` depending on task / model |\n| Whisker motion energy | Dynamic-behavior performance reported with `R2`; extracted Figure 8 text shows example IBL unaligned values up to roughly `0.80` |\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says description uses a causal window around movement and regression `R2` | For this project, dynamic outputs will be discretized after interpolation onto the shared stimulus-onset grid, because the target format requires categorical outputs and the user requested a single common alignment event. |\n\n### Final Understanding\n- The most defensible reference for the conversion mechanics is the Zhang 2025 repository’s preprocessing code, not every detail of the prose papers, because the papers and code are not fully internally consistent.\n- The data paper supplies the global dataset facts and core curation rules: 139 mice, 459 release sessions, 699 insertions, 621,733 units, RT and missing-event exclusions, and the definition of well-isolated neurons.\n- The Zhang repository supplies the concrete session-processing pipeline used for decoder-ready data:\n  - merge probes within session\n  - load spike-sorted clusters and expose QC metadata\n  - mask trials with missing critical events / out-of-range RT / no-choice / long trial\n  - interpolate behavior streams onto a fixed 20 ms grid\n  - create 2 s trial windows aligned to `stimOn_times`\n- The user’s decoder task explicitly overrides any remaining ambiguity by requiring stimulus-onset alignment and categorical outputs for all decoded variables.\n- Therefore the conversion plan for later steps will use:\n  - session-level units of analysis\n  - merged probes per session\n  - `clusters.metrics.label >= 1` as the neuron inclusion rule\n  - 20 ms bins over `[-0.5, 1.5]` s relative to stimulus onset\n  - trial masking based on the Zhang code\n  - left-then-right whisker fallback\n  - categorical remapping for choice / prior and discretization for wheel speed / whisker motion energy\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `spikes.times`, `spikes.clusters` from all probes in one session | `neural` | Merge probes, keep clusters with `clusters.metrics.label >= 1`, bin counts into 20 ms bins over `[-0.5, 1.5]` s relative to `stimOn_times`; store each trial as `(n_neurons, 100)` float array | `load_spiking_data`, `merge_probes`, behavior interpolation grid |\n| raw `probabilityLeft` block sequence | `input[1]` | Trial number within current probability block, computed on the original trial sequence, then repeated across all `choice == 0` are excluded by reference mask |\n| raw `probabilityLeft` | `output[1]` | Map `{0.2, 0.5, 0.8}` to `{0, 1, 2}` and repeat across all 100 bins | `bin_behaviors` loads this as `block` | User requested this categorical coding explicitly |\n| wheel stream | `output[2]` | Load wheel velocity, take absolute value to obtain speed, interpolate onto stimulus-onset grid, then discretize into 3 global bins | `load_target_behavior('wheel-speed')`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | Bins will be dataset-wide tertiles over valid aligned samples |\n| whisker motion energy stream | `output[3]` | Load left whisker motion energy; if unavailable, fall back to right; interpolate onto stimulus-onset grid; discretize into 3 global bins | `load_target_behavior('left-whisker-motion-energy' / 'right-whisker-motion-energy')`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | Bins will be dataset-wide tertiles over valid aligned samples |\n| session subject metadata | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | Build unique subject list from kept sessions and index each session into it | `bwm_release.csv` / session path metadata | Deterministic session order required |\n| cluster acronyms | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | Build global vocabulary of recorded acronyms and index each neuron | `clusters['acronym']` via `prepare_data` metadata | Use raw acronyms rather than Beryl remapping, because session export is neuron-level |\n| session / conversion settings | `metadata` | Store task description, 20 ms bin size, `stimOn_times` alignment, offsets, discretization edges, release/source notes | Project-defined | Include enough fields to reproduce the export |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Use 2D time-varying arrays for both `input` and `output`**: Static trial variables (`choice`, `prior`, `trial_number_in_block`) will be repeated across the 100-bin time axis so every trial has consistent `(d, T)` tensors.\n2. **Use the Zhang repository’s 2 s, 20 ms, stimulus-onset-aligned grid**: This matches the executable code and the user’s explicit alignment instruction, despite prose discrepancies in the papers.\n3. **Keep clusters with `label >= 1`**: This matches the data paper’s well-isolated-unit count exactly in the full local release (`75,708`) and keeps the dense export tractable while remaining anchored to an explicit QC field already present in the reference code.\n4. **Apply the reference trial mask before export**: Required events present, RT in `[0.08, 2.0]` s, `choice != 0`, and trial duration ≤ 10 s.\n5. **Require sessions to have all core streams needed by this task**: spikes, trials, wheel, and at least one whisker-motion-energy camera; afterwards, require at least 2 valid kept trials per session.\n6. **Use left whisker motion energy first, then right as fallback**: This exactly matches `bin_behaviors()` and maximizes session retention without inventing a new rule.\n7. **Discretize wheel speed and whisker motion energy using global tertiles**: Global edges preserve a common categorical meaning across sessions and should keep class balance better than fixed-width bins.\n8. **Compute trial number in block on the original unfiltered trial table**: Excluded trials should not renumber the latent block progression.\n9. **Map choice polarity using rewarded nonzero-contrast trials**: Local data confirm `choice == 1` corresponds to left and `choice == -1` to right, so export will use left=0, right=1.\n10. **Preserve raw region acronyms in `brain_regions`**: This avoids collapsing neurons across atlas levels and keeps the export closest to the cluster metadata.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Neural sanity check: for a chosen session/trial/neuron/bin, compare exported spike count to a direct count from raw `spikes.times` / `spikes.clusters` within the corresponding raw bin boundaries using `np.allclose()`.\n- [ ] Input sanity check: verify exported `time_since_stimulus_onset` exactly matches the constructed common grid and exported `trial_number_in_block` matches a direct raw-table block counter for selected trials.\n- [ ] Output sanity check 1: verify exported choice / prior values for selected trials against raw `trials.choice` and `trials.probabilityLeft` using direct remapping.\n- [ ] Output sanity check 2: verify aligned continuous wheel speed / whisker values at selected trial-bin locations against direct interpolation from raw streams before discretization, then verify discretized class assignments from stored global bin edges.\n- [ ] Session-count sanity check: compare numbers of kept sessions, trials, and neurons against the release table, the local cache, and the paper-reported totals to explain every reduction.\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n- Implemented `convert_data.py` with the required CLI:\n  - `python -u convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`\n  - `--sample` for 2 sessions\n  - `--full` for the full release manifest\n  - `--show-processing` to save 2 sessions completed without errors in 31.0 s\n\nCode inefficiencies identified:\n- The current implementation is serial across sessions, so the naive full conversion estimate is longer than the 15-minute target.\n- Trial-by-trial spike binning is the main hot path.\n\nCode speedups added:\n- Spike arrays are memory-mapped and only QC-passing spikes are materialized.\n- Spike counting uses `bincount2D` on pre-windowed spike subsets.\n- Behavior interpolation reuses a shared fixed grid per trial window.\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. Neural data is aligned to stimulus onset (`stimOn_times`).
